## 15. Finite-word uniformity and the remaining exact-profile gap

For fixed permitted epsilon,kappa, the whole construction works for
every finite nonnegative word and every admissible checkpoint subset.
The constants c,H,A are independent of its length, sums, and maximum
step. This is the finite-word uniformity that survived the audit.

It does not remove the following losses in(A5): the checkpoint
threshold, b_i-1 instead of b_i, c>1 on every ordinary sum,
max{epsilon b_i,A}, and the exact terminal1+2s_k. If all s_i>0 for
i<k, the diagnostic expression

    I=product_(i<k)(b_i/s_i)^2/(1+2s_k)

has logarithmic ratio to(A5)'s right side equal to

    Loss=2sum_i log[c+max{epsilon b_i,A}/s_i]
           -2sum_i log(1-1/b_i).

It is >=2k log c. The inequalities log(1+x)<=x for x>=0 and
-log(1-u)<=u/(1-u) for0<=u<1 give exactly

    Loss<=2k(c-1)+2sum_i max{epsilon b_i,A}/s_i
                         +2sum_i 1/(b_i-1).

For(A5a), relative to the analogous product with denominator s_i and
the same terminal1/[2(1+s_k)], the exact logarithmic loss is

    Loss_source=2sum_i log[c+(c a_sp+epsilon b_i)/s_i].

There is no identification of either diagnostic expression with B_n.
Zero ordinary sums make these diagnostics unavailable but do not
make the actual bounds undefined. Large epsilon b_i/s_i, the number
of factors, and the activation allowance are retained in all claims.
Pointwise parameter improvements are insufficient to control these
quantities uniformly over words and growing numbers of checkpoints.

The unresolved original affirmative target is the quantifier statement

    for every delta>0 there exists N(delta) such that
    for every n>=N(delta) and every h in[0,infinity)^n,
                     W_n(h)>=(1-delta)/B_n.

A sufficient next theorem for this restricted channel would replace
W_n(h) on the left by the supremum of(A4) over its allowed parameters
and checkpoint selections, including the Huber empty selection. The
supremum is finite, since each bound is realized by a legal instance
and W_n(h) is finite for fixed h. Neither that sufficient comparison
nor its negation has been established by this audit. A finite
checkpoint set can be maximized at fixed parameters; continuous
parameter optimization should be expressed as a supremum unless
attainment is separately proved.

The surviving result is precisely the finite-word construction in
Sections3--11, the matched certified-constant comparison in Section12,
and the finite paid obstruction in Sections13--14. The next asymptotic
comparison remains unproved. No formal acceptance, original-target
completion, or authorization for another round is inferred.

# A static smooth-convex witness above the benchmark on the original ruler

Status: COMPLETE JOINT ANALYTICAL CANDIDATE; UNVERIFIED.
Worker max4, round58. Assignment TASK.md; steering60da2cf01388bce5.
This is a prescribed-word lower witness, not a minimax resolution.

The only accepted mathematical input is fact04219554e41d9750, restricted
to its ORIGINAL benchmark ray and certified rational enclosure. The two
unverified source drafts c8fcf4ced5a34046 and8880a32b8502cf2f are provenance,
not predecessors: all geometry and scalar arguments needed here are
derived together below. No mathematical program, numerical evaluation,
parameter scan, executed recurrence, solver, formal gate, installation,
or sub-agent is used. Source originals are preserved.

## 1. Definitions and quantities to be realized

Set lambda=1+sqrt(2). For each integer m>=6 use exactly

    beta=1-1/m, c=lambda*(1+1/m), a=beta/c,
    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a*c^j,H_j), 0<=j<m,
    n_m=2^m-1, S_j=sum H_j, b_j=1+a*c^j.

Every child retains the SAME enclosing m. Only tau=1 is used anywhere
in this theorem. The finite lower bound below is proved whenever the
selected set is nonempty; this holds for every sufficiently large m.

Retain the ORIGINAL all-split benchmark:

    Omega(x,y)=(x+y+sqrt((x+y)^2+4*x*y))/2,
    G(x,y)=2*x+y/2+sqrt(y^2+8*x*y)/2,
    C_0=B_0=1,
    C_n=max_(0<=j<n) Omega(C_j,C_(n-j-1)),
    B_n=max_(0<=j<n) G(C_j,B_(n-j-1)), U_n^ZJ=1/B_n.

Every split index is an integer. Accepted fact04219554e41d9750 gives

    Z_1=lim_(m->infinity) B_(2^m-1)/lambda^m >0,
    lambda^(-r)*B_(2^r-1) <= Z_1
      <=lambda^(-r)*(sqrt(B_(2^r))+3/100)^2, r>=0.       (1)

Define the EXACT geometric parameters and costs

    K=14/5, E=1/100, kappa=K/m, epsilon=E/m,
    theta_sp=arctan(1/epsilon),
    chi=sqrt(1+epsilon^2)*exp(kappa*theta_sp), d=1-kappa^2,
    T=(exp(d*theta_sp/kappa)-1)/d,
    H=chi*[1+(1+kappa)*T]-1,
    A=epsilon+(H+1)/epsilon.                           (2)

Here 0<kappa<1 and 0<epsilon<=1/2. The symbol H without a subscript
denotes the geometric vertical allowance, not a physical word H_j.
Use the frozen selection rule, without altering any finite threshold:

    R_cut=max{A/epsilon,1+2/epsilon^2},
    J=max{1,ceil(log(R_cut/a)/log c)}.                  (3)

If J>=m the selection is empty and k=0. Otherwise select every FIRST
occurrence of the levels J,J+1,...,m-1 and no other physical step, and
put k=m-J.
Checkpoint i, 0<=i<k, has level J+i and value b^(i)=b_(J+i).
Its incoming ordinary word is denoted by Aword_i and its sum by s_i.
The terminal ordinary word is Aword_k, with sum s_k. In the empty case
Aword_0=H_m and s_0=S_m.

For the nonempty case define the scalar value to be realized:

    P_m = 1/[2*(1+S_(m-1))]
          * product_(i=0)^(k-1)
              [b^(i)/(chi*s_i+2*A+epsilon*b^(i))]^2.   (4)

For the empty case define P_m=1/[2*(1+S_m)].

Write q_rc=8+4sqrt(2) and theta=5*pi/(28*log lambda), and define

    L_joint=Z_1/(2*lambda)
         *exp[-1+(q_rc-14*pi/5-1/50)*(1-theta)].        (5)

The original normalized worst-case error is

    W_n(h)=sup 2*(f(x_n)-f(x_star))/(L*||x_0-x_star||^2), (6)

over all finite Euclidean dimensions, convex differentiable functions
with L-Lipschitz gradient and an attained minimum x_star, and x_0!=x_star,
under actual GD x_(t+1)=x_t-(h_t/L)*grad f(x_t). The final theorem appears
after its supporting construction and estimates. It concerns only this
prescribed family; the original minimax and uniform all-word target
remain open.

## 2. Original physical indices, sums and the paid threshold

Put zeta=a/(c-2)=beta/[c*(c-2)] and v_mass=c*(c-2). For x=1/m,

    v_mass=1+(4+2sqrt(2))*x+lambda^2*x^2>1>beta>0,
    0<zeta<1,
    S_j=zeta*c^j+(1-zeta)*2^j-1, 0<=j<=m.            (7)

Indeed S_0=0. Substitution into S_(j+1)=2*S_j+1+a*c^j gives the
next identity because zeta*(c-2)=a. This proves (7) by induction.

Index physical steps from0. The first occurrence of b_j is at
t_j=2^j-1. The prefix before it is H_j; for j<m-1 the word between
t_j and t_(j+1) is H_j; after t_(m-1) is H_(m-1). These statements follow inductively
from the displayed concatenation, preserving its fixed enclosing m.
Thus for J<m the decomposition is exactly

    H_J, b_J, H_J, b_(J+1), H_(J+1), b_(J+2), ...,
                    H_(m-2), b_(m-1), H_(m-1).        (8)

In particular s_0=S_J, s_i=S_(J+i-1) for1<=i<k, and s_k=S_(m-1).
There are k original selected steps and n_m-k ordinary steps. All
unselected copies remain in their actual physical positions.

From (3), a*c^J>=R_cut. Therefore EVERY selected b^(i) satisfies

    b^(i)>=2*(1+epsilon^(-2)),
    epsilon*b^(i)>=epsilon+A>A.                       (9)

The doubled source-format threshold is met at finite m, not merely
asymptotically. It also implies the smaller one-envelope threshold.

## 3. Independent-parameter arc and finite backward trajectories

We prove the local construction for arbitrary0<epsilon<=1/2 and
0<kappa<1, with the exact costs (2). No ordering kappa<=epsilon is used.
For0<=u<=theta_sp define

    e(u)=(cos u,-sin u), t(u)=(-sin u,-cos u),
    R(u)=chi*exp(-kappa*u),
    v(u)=R(u)*e(u)=(p(u),-q(u)), n(u)=e(u)+kappa*t(u).

Let K_arc be the convex hull of0 and this compact arc. Its endpoints
are v(0)=(chi,0) and v(theta_sp)=(epsilon,-1). Differentiation gives
p'=-R*(kappa*cos u+sin u)<0 and q'=R*(cos u-kappa*sin u). Therefore

    epsilon<=p(u)<=chi, q(u)>=0, q(u)/p(u)<=1/epsilon.  (10)

In particular q is allowed to exceed1. For w-u=Delta,

    <n(u),v(w)>/R(u)
       =exp(-kappa*Delta)*(cos Delta+kappa*sin Delta).

The derivative in Delta is
-(1+kappa^2)*exp(-kappa*Delta)*sin Delta. On the entire possible
interval [-theta_sp,theta_sp], the expression has maximum1 at0. Also
<n(u),0>=0<R(u). Thus n(u) supports K_arc at v(u). At v(0), the
additional vector (0,1) supports K_arc because q>=0.

Take ANY finite ordered list alpha_1,...,alpha_N>=0. Define backward
vectors r_j and selected arc points v_j as follows. Start with
r_N=v_N=(epsilon,-1) and angle u_N=theta_sp. Suppose r_j=(U_j,-Y_j)
is defined. With alpha=alpha_j, if

    Y_j<=kappa*[U_j+(alpha-1)*chi],                   (11)

set v_(j-1)=v(0), u_(j-1)=0, r_(j-1)=r_j+alpha*v(0).
Otherwise use the unique u in(0,u_j] satisfying

    <r_j,e(u)-t(u)/kappa>/R(u)=1-alpha,              (12)

and set u_(j-1)=u, v_(j-1)=v(u), r_(j-1)=r_j+alpha*v(u).
These are exact definitions, not executed root searches.

Here is existence, uniqueness and the projection proof. Inductively

    U_j>=epsilon, Y_j>=1, Y_j/U_j<=1/epsilon;           (13)

at every uncapped point there is a_j>=0 with

    r_j=R(u_j)*[(1+a_j)*e(u_j)+kappa*a_j*t(u_j)].       (14)

Initially a_N=0. In (11) let mu=U_j+(alpha-1)*chi. Then mu>0 and
r_(j-1)-v(0)=mu*n(0)+(0,kappa*mu-Y_j). Both are nonnegative
supporting-normal combinations, proving projection to v(0). The
inequality becomes Y_(j-1)<=kappa*(U_(j-1)-chi), so all earlier
backward entries are capped too.

If (11) fails, the point is not already capped. The left side of
(12) is1 at u_j by (14), less than1-alpha at0, and has derivative

    (kappa+kappa^(-1))*(U_j*cos u+Y_j*sin u)/R(u)>0.

This gives exactly one root, including alpha=0 with root u_j.
Representation (14) shows that the polar angle of r_j is at least
u_j, so <r_j,t(u)>=0 at that root. Equation (12) says that
r_j+(alpha-1)*v(u) is a nonnegative multiple of n(u): its t-component
is kappa times its e-component. This proves (14) for j-1 and the
projection property. Adding alpha*(p,-q) preserves (13) by (10).
Finally, if x-v has nonpositive dot product with every w-v in K_arc,
then ||x-w||^2>=||x-v||^2; thus all the supporting-normal arguments
indeed prove v_j=Pi_(K_arc)(r_j).

Define

    P=sum_(j=1)^N alpha_j*p(u_(j-1)),
    Q=sum_(j=1)^N alpha_j*q(u_(j-1)), S=sum_j alpha_j.

Then r_0=(epsilon+P,-1-Q), 0<=P<=chi*S and P>=epsilon*Q.
To bound Q independently of the word, consider two consecutive
uncapped angles u_old<=u_new and Delta=u_new-u_old. Taking the
t(u_old) component of r_old=r_new+alpha*v(u_old) gives exactly

    a_old=B_Delta+A_Delta*a_new,
    A_Delta=exp(-kappa*Delta)*(cos Delta+sin Delta/kappa),
    B_Delta=exp(-kappa*Delta)*sin Delta/kappa.          (15)

Both coefficients are nonnegative. With d=1-kappa^2>0,

    A_Delta'-(d/kappa)*A_Delta
       =-(1+kappa^(-2))*exp(-kappa*Delta)*sin Delta<=0.

Hence A_Delta<=exp(d*Delta/kappa), while B_Delta<=Delta/kappa and
1+d*B_Delta<=exp(d*Delta/kappa). It follows that
1+d*a_old<=exp(d*Delta/kappa)*(1+d*a_new). Telescoping from a_N=0
gives a_j<=T. By (14),

    Y_j=R(u_j)*[(1+a_j)*sin u_j+kappa*a_j*cos u_j]
       <=chi*[1+(1+kappa)*T]=1+H.

A cap leaves Y unchanged. Consequently

    0<=Q<=H, 0<=P<=chi*S, P>=epsilon*Q.               (16)

The needed geometry has now been proved without the false restriction
q<=1, without nonnegative horizontal normals, and without any per-step
unpriced error. The half-sum assembly below uses physical zero regions;
it does not require bounds on horizontal proximal coordinates.

## 4. Finite local functions and their zero regions

For a physical ordinary block of sum s and its physical checkpoint b,
apply Section3 to its entries divided by2. Write N for its length and
P,Q,r_j,v_j for that HALF-step construction. Set b_loc=b/2 and

    sigma=1/(chi*s/2+epsilon*b/2+A),
    z_circ=(1,0)-sigma*(epsilon+P,-1-Q),
    U=1-sigma*(P+epsilon*b_loc), Y=sigma*Q,
    V_fin={0,v(0),v_0,...,v_N},
    phi(z)=sigma*max_(v in V_fin)<v,z-z_circ>,
    f_loc(x)=min_z [phi(z)+||x-z||^2/2].              (17)

By (16), P<=chi*s/2 and Q<=H. Thus U>=0 and

    sigma*[P+epsilon+(1+Q)/epsilon]<=1.               (18)

The function phi is a finite nonnegative convex maximum of affine
functions. The minimizing objective defining f_loc is coercive and
strictly convex, so its proximal point z exists uniquely. If g=x-z,
the minimizing condition is g in partial phi(z). To justify both
directions directly, this subgradient inequality makes the cost at
any other w at least its cost at z plus ||w-z||^2/2. Conversely,
optimality at z, tested against z+t*(w-z) for0<t<1, and convexity of
phi imply phi(w)-phi(z)>=<g,w-z>-t*||w-z||^2/2. Letting t decrease
to0 proves the subgradient inequality. For two such pairs,
the two subgradient inequalities imply
||g-g'||^2<=<g-g',x-x'>. Moreover using z as trial point at x+w gives

    f_loc(x+w)<=f_loc(x)+<g,w>+||w||^2/2.

Using the actual new proximal point z' and the subgradient inequality
at z gives

    f_loc(x+w)>=f_loc(x)+<g,w>+||w-(z'-z)||^2/2.

These bounds prove differentiability with gradient g, global convexity,
and a1-Lipschitz gradient. Also f_loc>=0 and {f_loc=0}={phi=0}.

At x_j=z_circ+sigma*r_j, put z_j=x_j-sigma*v_j. Since the convex hull
of V_fin is contained in K_arc and includes v_j, its projection
inequality implies that sigma*v_j is an active subgradient of phi at
z_j. Thus grad f_loc(x_j)=sigma*v_j. In particular the original
ordered half-steps run exactly from x_0=(1,0) to
(U+sigma*epsilon*b_loc,Y); the step b_loc then reaches
(U,Y+sigma*b_loc).

For every nonzero v=(p,-q) in V_fin, (10) and (18) give

    <v,-z_circ>
      =-p+sigma*[p*(epsilon+P)+q*(1+Q)]<=0.

Therefore phi=f_loc=0 on {z_1<=0,z_2>=0}. Also b_loc>=1+epsilon^(-2)
by (9), so

    <v,(U,Y)-z_circ>=sigma*[(1-b_loc)*epsilon*p+q]<=0.

Increasing the second coordinate decreases this inner product. Hence

    f_loc(U,y)=0 for every y>=Y.                      (19)

Along the actual local path the first coordinate decreases and the
second increases. Before its checkpoint they obey x_1>=U and
0<=x_2<=Y. These are physical coordinate statements, valid independently
of the sign of the supporting normal. Zero steps, if present, retain
their original index and cause no exception to the construction.

## 5. One finite-dimensional static function and every physical update

First suppose J<m. Apply Section4 separately in advance to each actual
incoming block Aword_i and its checkpoint b^(i), i=0,...,k-1. All its symbols receive
the subscript i: sigma_i,P_i,Q_i,phi_i,f_i,U_i,Y_i,z_circ,i.
Define positive scales and nonnegative offsets by

    D_0=1, ell_0=0,
    G_i=sigma_i*b^(i)/2
       =b^(i)/(chi*s_i+2*A+epsilon*b^(i)),
    D_(i+1)=D_i*G_i,
    ell_(i+1)=D_i*Y_i,
    delta_tail=D_k/(1+s_k).                           (20)

The basis e_0,...,e_k is the standard orthonormal basis of R^(k+1).
For any delta>0 define the one-sided Huber function

    Hub_delta(z)=0                     if z<=0,
                 z^2/2                 if0<=z<=delta,
                 delta*z-delta^2/2      if z>=delta.

Its continuous derivative is max{0,min{z,delta}}, which is nondecreasing
and1-Lipschitz. The ACTUAL function used in the theorem is

    F_m(x)=(1/2)*sum_(i=0)^(k-1) D_i^2
                    *f_i((x_i-ell_i)/D_i,x_(i+1)/D_i)
           +(1/2)*Hub_(delta_tail)(x_k-ell_k).         (21)

This is a single function fixed before running GD. The finite lists
of planes, implicit backward roots, scales and shifts are mathematical
definitions determined by the entire prescribed word. No trajectory
is replayed or numerically solved to define (21). Its dimension is
exactly k+1=m-J+1 in this nonempty case, finite at every m. Each local
maximum has only finitely many planes even though the auxiliary arc is continuous.

The function is nonnegative and convex. Every ell_i>=0, so at x=0
each local input is in {z_1<=0,z_2>=0}, and the terminal term vanishes.
Therefore F_m(0)=min F_m=0. Each scaled local term before its factor1/2
has the1-smooth upper inequality on its two coordinates. Summing,

    F_m(x+w)<=F_m(x)+<grad F_m(x),w>
          +(1/4)*[sum_(i<k)(w_i^2+w_(i+1)^2)+w_k^2]
       <=F_m(x)+<grad F_m(x),w>+||w||^2/2.             (22)

Every coordinate is counted at most twice. This pays the overlap cost
once in the explicit coefficient1/2; no dimension factor remains.
For completeness, (22) implies1-Lipschitz gradient here: fix y and
apply (22) to V(x)=F_m(x)-<grad F_m(y),x> with w=-grad V(x).
Convexity makes y a minimizer of V. Consequently

    F_m(x)-F_m(y)-<grad F_m(y),x-y>
       >=||grad F_m(x)-grad F_m(y)||^2/2.              (23)

Add the reversed inequality and apply Cauchy--Schwarz to obtain the
Lipschitz bound. This argument does not assume a least smoothness
constant or an extra rescaling of the steps.

At the start of block i the claimed physical state is

    x_j=ell_j+D_j*U_j for j<i,
    x_i=ell_i+D_i,
    x_j=0 for j>i.                                    (24)

It is true initially at e_0. Follow the scaled local path in coordinates
i,i+1, leaving the other coordinates fixed. Before its checkpoint,

    x_i>=ell_i+D_i*U_i>=ell_i,
    0<=x_(i+1)<=D_i*Y_i=ell_(i+1).

For a previous component j<i, its first input is U_j and its second
is at least ell_(j+1)/D_j=Y_j. Thus (19) makes that component zero.
For a future component j>i, its first input is nonpositive and its
second input is0, so the other zero region makes it zero. The terminal
term is also zero until its activation. A differentiable nonnegative
function has zero gradient at any zero, since every directional
derivative there must vanish. Thus only the current component contributes.

Its global gradient is D_i/2 times the local gradient. A physical
step h_t therefore induces exactly the local step h_t/2. This proves
that the candidate local path is the actual GD path of (21) for every
ordinary step, in its given order. The physical checkpoint b^(i) then
leaves

    x_i=ell_i+D_i*U_i,
    x_(i+1)=D_i*(Y_i+sigma_i*b^(i)/2)
           =ell_(i+1)+D_(i+1).

This proves (24) for the next block. No component is removed or changed
at a transition; earlier components remain zero by the same inequality.
All steps, including all unselected occurrences of large levels, have
been included. Empty blocks and zero steps require no deletion.

During the terminal block let u be the sum of its executed physical
steps. Since0<=u<=s_k, its shifted moving coordinate is

    x_k-ell_k=D_k-delta_tail*u/2
       >=D_k-delta_tail*s_k/2
        =delta_tail*(1+s_k/2)>=delta_tail.            (25)

Every earlier component remains zero. The actual gradient equals
(delta_tail/2)*e_k at every terminal query and at the terminal point.
Hence direct substitution into (21) gives

    F_m(x_(n_m))=D_k^2/[4*(1+s_k)].                   (26)

Equations (20), (26), s_k=S_(m-1), and ||e_0-0||=1 prove EXACTLY (4).
For arbitrary L>0 set f_m=L*F_m. It is L-smooth and GD with physical
steps h_t/L has the identical path, while the normalization cancels L.

For the literal empty-selection case J>=m use k=0,D_0=1,ell_0=0,
and (21) reduces to F_m=Hub_(1/(1+S_m))/2 on R, starting at1.
The same terminal calculation gives normalized payoff1/[2*(1+S_m)].
Thus the exact selection rule also has a defined realization at every
earlier m>=6. No empty-case asymptotic is used in the final theorem.

## 6. All original interpolation conditions and the actual radius

For every ordered pair a,b of the actual query points, the terminal
point, and ANY minimizer, write g_a=grad F_m(x_a), f_a=F_m(x_a).
Inequality (23) with x=x_b,y=x_a yields

    Q_ab=f_a-f_b+<g_a,x_b-x_a>+||g_a-g_b||^2/2<=0.     (27)

Thus every original ordered smooth-convex interpolation row holds,
including both orientations of optimum and terminal rows. It is a
global function inequality, so no untested nonlocal pair or cycle
remains. For f_m=L*F_m the final squared-gradient term is divided by
2L, as required. The update equations were proved in Section5. Any
Gram matrix of these points and gradients is positive semidefinite
because they are actual vectors in R^(k+1): its quadratic form is
the squared norm of their indicated linear combination. Zero steps
give equal actual jets but still retain their physical positions.

The exact minimizer used in (4) is0, already proved to minimize F_m;
its radius is exactly ||e_0||=1. The true closest radius is also
accounted for explicitly. Write z_circ,i=(c_(i,1),c_(i,2)). The zero
set of the local phi_i, and hence f_i, is exactly

    z_1<=c_(i,1),
    epsilon*(z_1-c_(i,1))-(z_2-c_(i,2))<=0.           (28)

The endpoint planes make these inequalities necessary. They suffice
because each other arc slope (p,-q) is

    [(p-epsilon*q)/chi]*(chi,0)+q*(epsilon,-1),

a nonnegative combination by (10). Since (21) is a sum of nonnegative
functions, its whole minimum set is the explicit nonempty polyhedron

    C_min={x: x_i<=zeta_i,
                  epsilon*x_i-x_(i+1)<=epsilon*zeta_i-D_i*c_(i,2)
                    for0<=i<k;
               x_k<=ell_k},
    zeta_i=ell_i+D_i*c_(i,1).                         (29)

It contains0. For k>=1 the initial point e_0 violates the first inequality,
because zeta_0=1-sigma_0*(epsilon+P_0)<1. It is therefore outside this
closed minimum set. For k=0 the set is the nonpositive half-line and
the same conclusion holds from initial1. Its exact closest radius is

    rho_m=dist(e_0,C_min), 0<rho_m<=1.                 (30)

Attainment follows by minimizing squared distance on the nonempty
closed set after restricting to a sufficiently large compact ball;
strict convexity makes the projection unique. The radius about the
specified minimizer is1, while the true normalized value is exactly
P_m/rho_m^2>=P_m. Thus using the closest minimizer strengthens the final
bound; there is no assumption that0 is the closest minimizer.

## 7. Distinguishing the two geometric formulas and their radius costs

Define, on precisely the same selected positions, the scalar reference

    P5_m=1/(1+2*s_k)*product_(i<k)
               [(b^(i)-1)/(chi*s_i+max{epsilon*b^(i),A})]^2. (31)

This is the geometry draft's main mass-only formula(7), and the scalar
draft's F5. In that different one-envelope realization it is a lower
estimate on a payoff normalized by its closest-minimizer radius. Its
finite factors have b-1, a maximum allowance and terminal1/(1+2*s_k).

The ACTUAL function in this document is instead the half-sum (21).
Its specified-minimizer payoff P_m is the geometry draft's formula(8),
equivalently the scalar draft's F5a, with a_sp=2*A/chi. Its factors
have b, the additive allowance2*A and terminal1/[2*(1+s_k)]. Its
specified radius1 and closest radius(30) are properties of this same
actual function, proved above. The one-envelope radius formula from
the other draft is not assigned to (21).

For the remaining calculation (31) is just an explicitly defined
scalar reference. No assertion about the other draft's realization
is used as a premise. In particular one must not replace the ACTUAL
finite value P_m by P5_m. Section9 proves their ratio tends1 with
all accumulated losses paid. The source-format construction itself
has already been derived jointly in Sections3--6.

## 8. The exact activation phase and its logarithmic correction

The cost A in (2) satisfies the useful exact identity

    A=epsilon+sqrt(1+epsilon^2)/[epsilon*(1-kappa)]
           *[exp(theta_sp/kappa)-kappa*exp(kappa*theta_sp)]. (32)

Indeed 1+(1+kappa)*T=(exp(d*theta_sp/kappa)-kappa)/(1-kappa),
and chi*exp(d*theta_sp/kappa)=sqrt(1+epsilon^2)*exp(theta_sp/kappa).

With fixed K=14/5,E=1/100, the integral defining arctan gives
theta_sp=pi/2-E/m+O(m^(-3)). Factoring exp(theta_sp/kappa) from the bracket
in (32), its relative subtracted term is exponentially small in m.
The separate additive epsilon is also exponentially smaller than the
remaining positive term. The logarithms of sqrt(1+epsilon^2) and
1/(1-kappa) have bounded Taylor remainders of orders m^(-2) and m^(-1).
It follows, with constants independent of m, that

    log A=pi*m/(2*K)+log m-log E-E/K+O(1/m),
    log(A/epsilon)=pi*m/(2*K)+2*log m-2*log E-E/K+O(1/m). (33)

For explicit remainder justification, the arctan integral differs
from epsilon by at most epsilon^3/3; the log expansion follows from
integrating1/(1+t) on a fixed interval about0. After some fixed m,
theta_sp is bounded below by a positive constant, so the exponential
relative remainders are bounded by a fixed multiple of exp(-c_0*m)
for some c_0>0. Taking their logarithms is then justified by the
same integral bound. These estimates require no evaluated exponential.

The other quantity1+2/epsilon^2 in R_cut has polynomial growth, so
R_cut=A/epsilon for every sufficiently large m. Since
log c=log lambda+1/m+O(m^(-2)) and a->1/lambda, the exact ceiling gives

    J=theta*m+(2/log lambda)*log m+O(1),
    theta=pi/(2*K*log lambda)=5*pi/(28*log lambda).    (34)

The O(1) includes the exact rounding in (3); it is not an alteration
of the chosen cutoff. Section11 proves0<theta<220/343<1. Hence
1<=J<m for every sufficiently large m and k/m->1-theta>0.
The excluded first theta*m levels and the logarithmic correction are
the activation payment. They are retained in the exponent below.

## 9. Exact products and control of the entire growing index set

For J<m, (8)--(9) give the exact finite identities

    P5_m=1/(1+2*S_(m-1))
       *[a*c^J/(chi*S_J+epsilon*(1+a*c^J))]^2
       *product_(j=J+1)^(m-1)
          [a*c^j/(chi*S_(j-1)+epsilon*(1+a*c^j))]^2,  (35)

    P_m=1/[2*(1+S_(m-1))]
       *[(1+a*c^J)/(chi*S_J+2*A+epsilon*(1+a*c^J))]^2
       *product_(j=J+1)^(m-1)
          [(1+a*c^j)/(chi*S_(j-1)+2*A
                                    +epsilon*(1+a*c^j))]^2. (36)

The maximum in (31) is EXACTLY epsilon*b^(i), by (9). Products with
no factors are1. In particular a single selected checkpoint retains
the same prefix and tail H_(m-1).

Define the exact scalar corrections

    delta_l=[(1-zeta)*2^l-1]/(zeta*c^l),
    xi_j=1/(a*c^j),
    D_first=chi*(1+delta_J)+epsilon*(c-2)*(1+xi_J),
    D_bulk,j=chi*(1+delta_(j-1))+epsilon*v_mass*(1+xi_j),
    d_first=chi+epsilon*(c-2), d_bulk=chi+epsilon*v_mass.

The denominators D_first,D_bulk,j in this section are scalar
normalizations, distinct from the physical scales D_i in Section5.
Using a/zeta=c-2 and a*c/zeta=v_mass, (35) becomes

    P5_m=(c-2)^2*v_mass^[2*(k-1)]
       /[(1+2*S_(m-1))*D_first^2*product_(j>J)D_bulk,j^2]. (37)

The exceptional first factor is c-2, not v_mass. Replacing its prefix
by H_(J-1) would introduce an erroneous factor tending to lambda^2.

Here c stays in a compact subset of(2,infinity), a and zeta are bounded
away from0, and1-zeta=O(1/m). The EXACT errors obey

    sum_(l>=J)|delta_l|
      <=[(1-zeta)/zeta]*(2/c)^J/(1-2/c)
                         +(1/zeta)*c^(-J)/(1-1/c),
    sum_(j>=J)xi_j=c^(-J)/[a*(1-1/c)].                (38)

These sums extend to infinity only to upper-bound nonnegative error
terms; no infinite-dimensional function is constructed. By (34) the
bounds vanish exponentially in m. Since d_first,d_bulk>=chi>=1,

    |D_first/d_first-1|<=|delta_J|+epsilon*(c-2)*xi_J,
    |D_bulk,j/d_bulk-1|<=|delta_(j-1)|+epsilon*v_mass*xi_j.

The sum of these bounds, with delta_J counted twice if necessary,
vanishes by (38). Each is eventually at most1/2. The integral formula
for log gives |log(1+t)|<=2|t| there. Therefore replacing all normalized
denominators simultaneously by d_first,d_bulk incurs a total logarithmic
error tending0, explicitly controlled by (38). This is a bound on the
whole product, not a pointwise o(1) multiplied by k without control.

It remains to pay every difference between the actual P_m and P5_m.
For each selected level j, let s^(j)=S_J when j=J and S_(j-1) otherwise,
and put

    V_j=2*A/(chi*s^(j)+epsilon*b_j),
    tau_tail=(1+2*S_(m-1))/[2*(1+S_(m-1))] in(0,1).

The exact ratio is

    P_m/P5_m=tau_tail
                   *product_(j=J)^(m-1)[(1+xi_j)/(1+V_j)]^2. (39)

For every sufficiently large m, (38) gives S_l>=zeta*c^l/2 for l>=J.
The EXACT cutoff (3) gives A*c^(-J)<=epsilon*a. Thus

    sum_j V_j
      <=[4*A/(zeta*chi)]*c^(-J)*[1+1/(1-1/c)]
      <=[4*epsilon*(c-2)/chi]*[1+1/(1-1/c)].           (40)

The separate1 in the bracket pays the first prefix; the geometric
series pays every later gap, including the repeated H_J. Since
-log tau_tail=log[1+1/(1+2*S_(m-1))]<=1/(1+2*S_(m-1)) and
0<=log(1+t)<=t for t>=0, (38)--(40) give

    |log(P_m/P5_m)|
      <=1/(1+2*S_(m-1))
        +2*c^(-J)/[a*(1-1/c)]
        +[8*epsilon*(c-2)/chi]*[1+1/(1-1/c)] ->0.      (41)

This pays the source-format additive2*A, b versus b-1, and its distinct
terminal factor. Its last term is O(1/m); the other terms decay
exponentially. No checkpoint loss is silently dropped.

## 10. The exact limit, with the first record and terminal block retained

Set gamma=4+2sqrt(2), q_rc=2*gamma. Elementary expansions with uniform
remainders, obtained from the log integral as in Section8, give

    log v_mass=gamma/m+O(m^(-2)),
    log chi=pi*K/(2*m)+O(m^(-2)),
    log(chi+epsilon*v_mass)=(pi*K/2+E)/m+O(m^(-2)).   (42)

The quantities depend only on m, not on the selected level. There
are at most m factors, so summing all O(m^(-2)) remainders gives
O(1/m). Equations (37)--(38) therefore give

    P5_m=(c-2)^2/[(1+2*S_(m-1))*d_first^2]
                 *[v_mass/d_bulk]^[2*(k-1)]*exp(o(1)). (43)

The o(1) is bounded by the explicit sums in (38). Moreover
d_first->1, c-2->lambda^(-1), zeta->1, and (7) gives

    lambda^m/(1+2*S_(m-1)) -> lambda*exp(-1)/2.        (44)

To verify (44), divide S_(m-1) by zeta*c^(m-1); its error delta_(m-1)
tends0 by (38). Then use (lambda/c)^m=(1+1/m)^(-m)->exp(-1).
The logarithm of the growing power in (43) tends, by (34),(42), to

    (q_rc-pi*K-2*E)*(1-theta).                       (45)

The replacement k=(1-theta)*m+O(log m) contributes O(log(m)/m) to
this logarithm. Combined with (1), (41), and (43)--(45), this proves

    lim B_(n_m)*P_m
      =Z_1/(2*lambda)*exp[-1+(q_rc-pi*K-2*E)*(1-theta)]
      =L_joint.                                     (46)

Every geometric/product error has been summed: it is O(log(m)/m)
apart from exponentially decaying terms before applying the accepted
benchmark convergence. Only the already accepted convergence in (1)
is needed, so no new convergence rate for B_n is assumed or computed.
The factor1/(2*lambda) includes the exceptional first record and the
true terminal child. The enclosing beta cancels exactly through
a/zeta=c-2; it was not separately approximated in each of k factors.

## 11. An exact strict margin from accepted endpoints and rational bounds

The certified table fixed in Section8 of accepted04219554e41d9750 is
/opt/danus/runtime/projects/gd-exact-profile-20260926/research/audit_c78/input_interval_table.csv,
SHA2562a7585bc0bade9f85461456c13d60bbe618f85c7cc816e1ddc5b4c1a1d611e68.
Its denominator and saved lower endpoint are

    D80=1208925819614629174706176,
    L1023=19205851554343006584447327614<=D80*B_1023,
    15880*D80<L1023.                                 (47)

This is a read of the accepted saved endpoint, not a new evaluation
of the benchmark recurrence or its certificate. The table hash was
matched; no numerical or exact recurrence was executed.

Squaring gives140/99<sqrt(2)<99/70, since19600<19602 and9800<9801.
Successive multiplication gives lambda^10=3363+2378*sqrt(2)<6727.
Thus (1) at r=10 and (47) yield

    Z_1>15880/6727>59/25,                             (48)

where15880*25=397000>396893=6727*59. Also lambda<17/7, since
sqrt(2)<10/7. Hence

    Z_1/(2*lambda)>413/850.                           (49)

We require no upper estimate for Z_1. For the remaining constants,
polynomial division and integration give

    22/7-pi=integral_0^1 x^4*(1-x)^4/(1+x^2) dx>0.

Set t=(lambda-1)/(lambda+1)=sqrt(2)-1>41/99. Integrating the first
two nonnegative terms of1/(1-t^2) gives

    log lambda=2*integral_0^t du/(1-u^2)
      >2*(t+t^3/3)>2548888/2910897>7/8,
    8*2548888-7*2910897=14825>0.                      (50)

Consequently0<theta=5*pi/(28*log lambda)<220/343, as used in (34),
and1-theta>123/343. With K=14/5,E=1/100,

    q_rc-pi*K-2*E
      >8+560/99-44/5-1/50=23941/4950>0.

Therefore the exponent in (46) satisfies

    -1+(q_rc-pi*K-2*E)*(1-theta)
      >-1+(23941/4950)*(123/343)
       =1246893/1697850>73/100.                      (51)

Positive terms of the exponential series, used as a textual inequality,
give

    exp(73/100)>1+73/100+(73/100)^2/2+(73/100)^3/6
               =12367717/6000000.

Combining with (49),

    L_joint>5107867121/5100000000>1001/1000.           (52)

The final comparison is exact: on denominator5100000000, subtracting
1001/1000 leaves numerator2767121>0. All arithmetic in this section
is a displayed symbolic proof using an already accepted endpoint;
no arithmetic program or numerical evaluation was run.

## 12. Complete match to every scalar-interface condition

This section spells out the interface from Section9 of the scalar
source as a consequence of the JOINT proof, not as a conditional arrow.

| Required item | Actual item proved here |
| --- | --- |
| Original word and indices | tau=1, same m in every child, n_m=2^m-1, t_j=2^j-1; (7)--(9) |
| One static function | f_m=L*F_m from (17),(20),(21), dimension k+1 |
| Effective chi | Exactly (2); m*log chi->7*pi/5 by (42) |
| Effective epsilon | Exactly1/(100*m), without an added tilt coefficient |
| Effective activation A | Exactly (2),(32); log A=(5*pi/28)*m+log m-log E-E/K+O(1/m) |
| Physical threshold | Exactly2*(1+10000*m^2), met by (9) |
| Exact selection cutoff | Exactly (3), including its maximum, ceiling, minimum level1 and empty case |
| Actual transfers | D_(i+1)=D_i*b^(i)/(chi*s_i+2*A+epsilon*b^(i)), (20) |
| Actual terminal factor | 2*F_m(x_n)=D_k^2/[2*(1+S_(m-1))], (26) |
| All accumulated scalar differences | Explicit bounds (38),(40),(41),(42); no pointwise-only error |
| Smoothness and overlap | L-smooth by (22)--(23); coefficient1/2 paid in local half-steps and terminal factor |
| Minimum and initial radius | Actual minimizer0, x_0=e_0, specified radius1; true rho_m in(0,1], (29)--(30) |
| All original interpolation conditions | Actual GD and every ordered row (27), real PSD Gram |

The source-format alternative in that Section9 is satisfied with exact
transfers, its exact terminal factor, radius multiplier r_m=1 and no
additional per-transfer factor. Thus its limiting loss is ell_loss=0.
The costs are C_eff=7*pi/5, alpha_eff=5*pi/28 and
theta=alpha_eff/log lambda. The threshold grows polynomially, so it
meets the required activation exponent. The sufficient radius/loss
budget is r*exp(2*ell_loss)=1<1001/1000, where r=lim r_m=1.

One can also match its PRIMARY conditions G2--G3 exactly, rather than
confusing the two finite terminal factors. Let tau_tail,V_j be those
in (39)--(40). On the same physical trajectory define bookkeeping
scales hatD_i=D_i for i<k and hatD_k=sqrt(tau_tail)*D_k. Then

    2*F_m(x_(n_m))=hatD_k^2/(1+2*S_(m-1)).            (53)

For i<k-1 set eta_i=1+V_(J+i), and set
eta_(k-1)=(1+V_(m-1))/sqrt(tau_tail). They are all at least1.
Because b_j=(b_j-1)*(1+xi_j), the exact transfers (20) imply

    hatD_(i+1)>=hatD_i*(b^(i)-1)
       /[(chi*s_i+max{epsilon*b^(i),A})*eta_i].       (54)

This also covers k=1. Every eta is attached to a scale on the actual
path; the final adjustment changes bookkeeping, not the function,
trajectory or word. Equations (40)--(41) give

    0<=sum_i log eta_i
       <=sum_j V_j-(1/2)*log tau_tail ->0.             (55)

Thus G1 is Section5 and (27); G2 is (54)--(55); G3 is (53) with
L restored; G4 is dist(e_0,argmin f_m)^2<=1=hatD_0^2. All four
conditions are proved with the actual costs and accumulated losses.
The source construction is not falsely asserted to dominate P5_m
without these finite corrections.

## 14. Final prescribed-word theorem and eventual strict separation

THEOREM (joint analytical candidate; UNVERIFIED). Let lambda=1+sqrt(2).
For each integer m>1 set beta_m=1-1/m, c_m=lambda*(1+1/m),
a_m=beta_m/c_m, H_0=(), and

    H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a_m*c_m^j,H_j), 0<=j<m,

keeping the SAME m in all children; let n_m=2^m-1. Let B_n and U_n^ZJ
be exactly the original all-split benchmark defined in Section1.
Use the fixed geometric parameters kappa_m=14/(5*m),
epsilon_m=1/(100*m), costs (2), and the exact cutoff (3), selecting
only the first-occurrence records at levels J,...,m-1.

There exists a finite integer M such that for EVERY integer m>=M and
EVERY L>0 the function f_m=L*F_m constructed in (17),(20),(21) has all
the following properties. Its finite dimension is m-J+1. It is convex,
differentiable and has L-Lipschitz gradient. It attains its minimum0
at the actual minimizer0. Starting from the explicit unit vector e_0,
actual GD with EVERY step of H_m, in its original physical order and
with steps h_t/L, has normalized terminal error

    2*(f_m(x_(n_m))-f_m(0))/(L*||e_0-0||^2)
       =P_m>(1+1/1000)*U_(n_m)^ZJ.                    (56)

The exact normalized value P_m is (4), and its exact benchmark-scaled
limit is

    lim B_(n_m)*P_m
      =Z_1/(2*lambda)*exp[-1+
          (8+4sqrt(2)-14*pi/5-1/50)
                *(1-5*pi/(28*log lambda))]
      >5107867121/5100000000>1001/1000.               (57)

The closest-minimizer radius is rho_m=dist(e_0,C_min) in(0,1], with
C_min explicitly given by (29). Its true-distance normalized payoff
is P_m/rho_m^2>=P_m. In particular

    liminf B_(n_m)*W_(n_m)(H_m)>=L_joint>1001/1000.     (58)

PROOF. Section8 establishes J<m for all sufficiently large m, with
the exact paid cutoff. Sections3--6 construct the single static
function, prove its claimed smoothness, minimum, both radius statements,
every physical update, every ordered interpolation row, and the exact
payoff (4). Sections9--10 bound all accumulated errors and give (57).
Section11 proves its strict rational lower enclosure. Section12 checks
that no cost is lost in joining the geometry to the scalar estimate.

To pass from the limit to the quantifier in (56), put
eta_margin=(L_joint-1001/1000)/2>0. By (46), after one finite depth
every subsequent m satisfies |B_(n_m)*P_m-L_joint|<eta_margin. Hence
B_(n_m)*P_m>(L_joint+1001/1000)/2>1001/1000. Enlarge that depth to
include m>=6 and J<m. Multiplication by L leaves the path and payoff
unchanged, so this single M works for every L>0. No numerical value
for M is asserted. The closest-radius comparison gives (58).

This completes the assigned prescribed-family derivation as ONE
UNVERIFIED object. It establishes no optimality over checkpoint sets,
no negative resolution of the minimax problem, and no uniform all-n,
all-nonnegative-word affirmative theorem. The original target remains
OPEN; no new verified fact or automatic worker-round renewal is claimed.

# Decoupling spiral curvature from checkpoint tolerance

Status: JOINT UNVERIFIED analytical candidate, max4 round57. This is the
single mechanism authorized by TASK.md and master_guidance06950132d6f04c7a.
No mathematical program, numerical experiment, parameter scan, solver,
formal submission, or sub-agent was used. All new assertions and all
needed baseline lemmas are proved jointly below. None is an accepted fact.

The frozen baseline is cdd7f057606aeb18, SHA256
366cd6a12c8e4f56cf3c875c104f3e9bfdc185bf26c955e4f7e83d5bef7b607c.
It is not a premise. The present object is a new complete range-extension
draft, not an edit of that baseline or a report of its independent audit.

## 1. Result, domain, and conventions

The ordering kappa<=epsilon is not needed for this support arc and its
global assembly. It was used to obtain two sufficient coordinate claims:
q(theta)<=1 and u_j>=p_j. Both can be replaced. The vertical proximal
buffer is unchanged because y_j>=q_j. The negative horizontal normal is
paid by the checkpoint threshold, explicitly as follows.

Take independently

    0<epsilon<=1/2,  kappa>0.

Define the following word-independent constants. The fresh symbol
theta_sp avoids the unrelated accepted benchmark symbol Theta.

    theta_sp=arctan(1/epsilon),
    c_sp=sqrt(1+epsilon^2) exp(kappa theta_sp),
    d_sp=1-kappa^2,
    T_sp=(exp(d_sp theta_sp/kappa)-1)/d_sp,  0<kappa<1,
    T_sp=theta_sp/kappa,                    kappa>=1,
    H_sp=c_sp[1+(1+kappa)T_sp]-1,
    A_sp=epsilon+(H_sp+1)/epsilon,
    a_sp=2A_sp/c_sp.                                      (1)

The formula for T_sp agrees continuously at kappa=1. Define also

    qmax_sp=1,                                      kappa<=epsilon,
    qmax_sp=sqrt((1+epsilon^2)/(1+kappa^2))
             exp(kappa[theta_sp-arctan(1/kappa)]),  kappa>epsilon,
    N_sp=max{0,kappa qmax_sp-epsilon},
    B_sp=1+max{epsilon^(-2),N_sp/epsilon}.                (2)

The number qmax_sp is the EXACT vertical maximum of the unchanged arc;
N_sp is a uniform upper bound on the horizontal projection deficit,
not a claimed sharp maximum of that deficit. The main construction is
valid for all parameters in the displayed domain with selected steps
b_i>=B_sp. In particular,

    B_sp=1+epsilon^(-2) whenever 0<kappa<=1.              (3)

More generally the same threshold is certified whenever
epsilon N_sp<=1. Thus the entire rectangular range
0<epsilon<=1/2, 0<kappa<=1 works, including epsilon<kappa, with exactly
the old formulas for c_sp,H_sp,A_sp when kappa<1. At kappa=1 use (1).
The general kappa>1 extension pays the displayed threshold if needed.
No assertion is made that (2) is a necessary threshold or a maximal
unchanged-threshold range.

Let h=(h_0,...,h_(n-1)) be ANY finite nonnegative word of its ORIGINAL
physical length n. Write it in its original order as

    (block_0,b_0,block_1,b_1,...,block_(k-1),b_(k-1),block_k),

where s_i is the sum of the possibly empty ordinary block_i. No upper
bound is imposed on any ordinary or selected step; zeros are retained.
Let W_n(h) be the supremum of

    2(f(x_n)-min f)/(L||x_0-x_star||^2)

over finite-dimensional convex differentiable L-smooth objectives with
an attained minimum, minimizers x_star, and x_0!=x_star, for physical GD
x_(t+1)=x_t-(h_t/L)grad f(x_t). This is W=2R.

For each i<k the backward construction in Section3, applied to the actual
ordered block_i, yields P_i,Q_i with

    0<=P_i<=c_sp s_i,  0<=Q_i<=H_sp,  P_i>=epsilon Q_i.

Put

    M_i=P_i+max{epsilon b_i,epsilon+(1+Q_i)/epsilon},
    sigma_i=1/M_i,
    D_0=1, ell_0=0,
    D_(i+1)=D_i sigma_i(b_i-1),
    ell_(i+1)=D_i sigma_i(1+Q_i).                        (4)

For k>=1 and b_i>=B_sp the finite max-affine function J_sp constructed
in Section5 defines ONE objective f=env_1 J_sp on R^(k+1), fixed before
the run, starting at x_0=e_0, with

    min f=0,
    2f(x_n)=D_k^2/(1+2s_k),
    dist(e_0,argmin f)^2
       =sigma_0^2[(epsilon+P_0)^2+(1+Q_0)^2].           (5)

All directed smooth-convex interpolation rows, including optimum and
terminal rows, hold for its actual data. Its exact closest-distance
payoff is

    (b_0-1)^2 / {[(epsilon+P_0)^2+(1+Q_0)^2](1+2s_k)}
       times product_(i=1)^(k-1) [(b_i-1)/M_i]^2.        (6)

This is bounded below by the fully explicit mass-only expression

    1/(1+2s_k) times product_(i=0)^(k-1)
       [(b_i-1)/(c_sp s_i+max{epsilon b_i,A_sp})]^2.      (7)

The construction's exact payoff (6) and the lower estimate (7) are
different objects. For k=0 the usual one-dimensional hinge envelope
has exact closest-distance payoff 1/(1+2s_0).

A second realization of this SAME arc gives the source-format statement
for the entire parameter domain kappa>0, 0<epsilon<=1/2, with threshold

    b_i>=2(1+epsilon^(-2)),

and no N_sp threshold. Its specified-minimizer payoff is EXACTLY

    1/[2(1+s_k)] times product_(i=0)^(k-1)
       [b_i/(c_sp(a_sp+s_i)+epsilon b_i)]^2.             (8)

Here the specified minimizer is 0 at initial distance1. Section7 also
gives an exact finite algebraic formula for that realization's TRUE
closest-minimizer radius, so the possible distinction is not hidden.
Its true-distance payoff is (8) divided by that squared radius, which
is positive and at most1. The main realization already has the simple
closed radius (5). No radius inflation is used in either bound.

For every L>0 replace the displayed objective by L times that objective.
The step h_t/L then produces the same physical trajectory and payoff.
Neither dimension growth nor smoothness normalization introduces an
unreported factor.

## 2. The unchanged support arc: exact extrema and normals

For 0<=theta<=theta_sp set

    e_sp(theta)=(cos theta,-sin theta),
    t_sp(theta)=(-sin theta,-cos theta),
    R_sp(theta)=c_sp exp(-kappa theta),
    v_sp(theta)=R_sp(theta)e_sp(theta)=(p(theta),-q(theta)),
    n_sp(theta)=e_sp(theta)+kappa t_sp(theta).

Let K_sp be the convex hull of 0 and this compact arc. Its endpoints
are (c_sp,0) and (epsilon,-1). Direct differentiation gives

    p'=-R_sp(kappa cos theta+sin theta)<0,
    q'= R_sp(cos theta-kappa sin theta).

Thus epsilon<=p<=c_sp and q>=0 for every kappa>0. Also
q/p=tan theta<=1/epsilon. The maximum of q is at the endpoint if
kappa<=epsilon, and otherwise at theta_c=arctan(1/kappa). This proves
the exact formula (2). In the latter case qmax_sp>1. More explicitly,

    log qmax_sp = integral_(epsilon)^kappa
                         (kappa-t)/(1+t^2) dt,
    (kappa-epsilon)^2/[2(1+kappa^2)]
       <=log qmax_sp<=(kappa-epsilon)^2/2.              (9)

These follow by differentiating the explicit logarithm in its lower
parameter and integrating. They quantify the genuine overshoot; it
is not discarded as a per-checkpoint asymptotic error.

For delta=phi-theta in [-theta_sp,theta_sp],

    <n_sp(theta),v_sp(phi)>/R_sp(theta)
       =exp(-kappa delta)(cos delta+kappa sin delta).

Its derivative is -(1+kappa^2)exp(-kappa delta)sin delta. The maximum
is1 at delta=0, since theta_sp<pi/2. Moreover
<n_sp(theta),0>=0<R_sp(theta). Hence n_sp(theta) is an outward
supporting normal to K_sp at v_sp(theta), for EVERY positive kappa.
At v_sp(0), the vector (0,1) is also supporting, because q>=0.

The horizontal component of the actual normal is cos theta-kappa
sin theta. It decreases from1 to (epsilon-kappa)/sqrt(1+epsilon^2).
It is negative exactly when kappa>epsilon and theta>theta_c. The
vertical component is -sin theta-kappa cos theta<0 throughout the arc.
These signs, not an assumed coordinate monotonicity, are used below.

## 3. Arbitrary nonnegative ordinary words and a uniform displacement

Fix an ordered ordinary block alpha_1,...,alpha_m, with alpha_j>=0.
Set r_m=v_m=v_sp(theta_sp)=(epsilon,-1). Work backward. Given
r_j=(u_j,-y_j), with projected arc point v_j=v_sp(theta_j), let
alpha=alpha_j. If

    y_j<=kappa[u_j+(alpha-1)c_sp],                       (10)

put v_(j-1)=v_sp(0) and r_(j-1)=r_j+alpha v_sp(0).
Otherwise choose the unique theta in (0,theta_j] solving

    <r_j,e_sp(theta)-kappa^(-1)t_sp(theta)>/R_sp(theta)
       =1-alpha,                                       (11)

and set v_(j-1)=v_sp(theta), r_(j-1)=r_j+alpha v_(j-1).
These are exact implicit definitions with proved existence and
uniqueness, not executed root searches or numerical assumptions.

We prove backward by induction that v_j=Pi_(K_sp)(r_j), that

    u_j>=epsilon,  y_j>=1,  y_j/u_j<=1/epsilon,          (12)

and that before a cap, including the initial endpoint, there is t_j>=0
such that

    r_j=R_sp(theta_j)[(1+t_j)e_sp(theta_j)
                                  +kappa t_j t_sp(theta_j)]. (13)

At j=m these statements hold with t_m=0. In case (10), put
mu=u_j+(alpha-1)c_sp. Then mu>=y_j/kappa>0, and

    r_(j-1)-v_sp(0)=mu n_sp(0)+(0,kappa mu-y_j).

Both coefficients of these supporting normals are nonnegative. This
proves projection at the cap. It also gives
y_(j-1)<=kappa(u_(j-1)-c_sp), so every earlier backward step is capped.

If (10) fails, the current point cannot already be capped. The left
side of (11) is1 at theta_j by (13), and is
(u_j-y_j/kappa)/c_sp<1-alpha at theta=0. Its derivative is

    (kappa+kappa^(-1))(u_j cos theta+y_j sin theta)/R_sp(theta)>0.

There is exactly one root in (0,theta_j]. Since u_j,y_j are positive,
(13) shows that the polar angle of r_j is at least theta_j; therefore
<r_j,t_sp(theta)>=0 at the root. Equation (11) says precisely that
r_j+(alpha-1)v_sp(theta) is a nonnegative multiple of n_sp(theta).
This proves projection and (13) at the preceding index. Finally,
adding alpha(p,-q), with p>=epsilon and 0<=q/p<=1/epsilon, preserves
(12). This also covers alpha=0 without deleting that physical entry.

Let

    P=sum_j alpha_j p(theta_(j-1)),
    Q=sum_j alpha_j q(theta_(j-1)), S=sum_j alpha_j.

Then r_0=(epsilon+P,-1-Q), P<=c_sp S, and P>=epsilon Q. We next
bound Q independently of both the length and the size of the steps.

For two consecutive uncapped angles theta_old<=theta_new, put
Delta=theta_new-theta_old. Taking the t_sp(theta_old) component in
the exact relation r_old=r_new+alpha v_sp(theta_old) gives

    t_old=B(Delta)+A(Delta)t_new,
    A(Delta)=exp(-kappa Delta)(cos Delta+sin Delta/kappa),
    B(Delta)=exp(-kappa Delta)sin Delta/kappa.            (14)

Both coefficients are nonnegative. With d=1-kappa^2, direct
differentiation gives

    A'-(d/kappa)A
       =-(1+kappa^(-2))exp(-kappa Delta)sin Delta<=0.

Thus A<=exp(d Delta/kappa), for all kappa>0. If kappa<1 then
B<=Delta/kappa and 1+dB<=exp(d Delta/kappa). Consequently

    1+d t_old<=exp(d Delta/kappa)(1+d t_new).

Telescoping from the endpoint gives
t_j<=[exp(d(theta_sp-theta_j)/kappa)-1]/d<=T_sp.
If kappa>=1, A<=1 and B<=Delta/kappa, so (14) instead gives
t_j<=(theta_sp-theta_j)/kappa<=T_sp. No sign reversal in a
negative-d inequality is used. At an uncapped point,

    y_j=R_sp(theta_j)[(1+t_j)sin theta_j
                                    +kappa t_j cos theta_j]
       <=c_sp[1+(1+kappa)T_sp]=1+H_sp.

A cap leaves y unchanged. Hence 0<=Q=y_0-1<=H_sp, as asserted.

Two more bounds repair precisely the places that lost kappa<=epsilon.
At every uncapped index, (13) gives

    y_j-q_j=R_sp(theta_j)t_j(sin theta_j+kappa cos theta_j)>=0. (15)

The same inequality holds at a cap because q_j=0. If the horizontal
normal is nonnegative, u_j-p_j>=0. Otherwise theta_j>theta_c,
p_j<=p(theta_c)=kappa qmax_sp, and u_j>=epsilon. At a cap u_j>=p_j.
Therefore the uniform bound is

    u_j-p_j>=-N_sp.                                    (16)

For clarity, (16) is a bound on the ACTUAL normal displacement, even
though its proof uses the separate bounds on u_j and p_j. It neither
asserts a nonnegative horizontal normal nor absorbs an error per step.

To prove (3), when kappa<=epsilon the deficit N_sp is0. Otherwise
qmax_sp decreases in epsilon, and

    qmax_sp<= exp(kappa arctan kappa)/sqrt(1+kappa^2).

The logarithmic derivative of the right side in kappa is arctan kappa,
so for kappa<=1 it is at most exp(pi/4)/sqrt(2)<2. One elementary
verification of the last strict inequality uses pi<4 and
exp(1)<=5/2+2/9=49/18<2sqrt(2), obtained by bounding the factorial
series from its third term by a geometric series of ratio1/4.
It follows that N_sp<2<=1/epsilon. This proves (3).

## 4. Finite local planes and both required zero regions

Suppress the block index. For b>=1+epsilon^(-2), take any positive
sigma such that

    sigma(P+epsilon b)<=1,
    sigma[P+epsilon+(1+Q)/epsilon]<=1.                  (17)

Define

    z_circ=(1,0)-sigma(epsilon+P,-1-Q),
    U=1-sigma(P+epsilon b)>=0, Y=sigma Q,
    V_fin={0,v_sp(0),v_0,...,v_m},
    phi(z)=sigma max_(v in V_fin)<v,z-z_circ>.           (18)

The finite convex hull of V_fin is contained in K_sp and includes
every v_j. The projection inequalities from Section3 therefore hold
on this finite hull. At x_j=z_circ+sigma r_j and z_j=x_j-sigma v_j,
the plane with slope sigma v_j is active in phi. Indeed, for every
v in V_fin, <r_j-v_j,v-v_j><=0. Thus sigma v_j belongs to the
subdifferential of phi at z_j. The proximal minimizer of phi at x_j
is exactly z_j, and grad(env_1 phi)(x_j)=sigma v_j.

For every nonzero v=(p,-q) in V_fin, p>0 and 0<=q<=p/epsilon. Hence

    <v,-z_circ>
       =-p+sigma[p(epsilon+P)+q(1+Q)]
       <=-p+sigma p[P+epsilon+(1+Q)/epsilon]<=0.

It follows that phi=0 on {z_1<=0,z_2>=0}. At (U,Y),

    <v,(U,Y)-z_circ>=sigma[(1-b)epsilon p+q]<=0.         (19)

So phi(U,y)=0 whenever y>=Y. Both statements remain true of its
nonnegative envelope, whose zero set is exactly that of phi.

The local trajectory starts at (1,0), follows every actual ordinary
step, and ends that block at (U+sigma epsilon b,Y), with gradient
sigma(epsilon,-1). The step b then reaches (U,Y+sigma b). Physical
first coordinates decrease and second coordinates increase throughout;
in particular x_(j),1>=U and 0<=x_(j),2<=Y before the checkpoint.
This fact uses only p>0, q>=0, not the sign of a normal.

For the ONE-envelope global assembly we additionally need bounds at
the local proximal points. Monotonicity of backward y gives
1<=y_j<=1+Q. Together with (15),

    0<= (z_j)_2=sigma[1+Q-y_j+q_j]<=sigma(1+Q).        (20)

The upper bound is EXACTLY the old buffer: the overshoot qmax_sp-1
causes no added loss, because y_j-q_j>=0. Similarly, by (16),

    (z_j)_1-U=sigma[epsilon(b-1)+u_j-p_j]
       >=sigma[epsilon(b-1)-N_sp]>=0                  (21)

whenever b>=B_sp. This is the sole additional threshold used by that
assembly. The source-format sum assembly uses physical points only
and consequently needs (19), but not (21).

## 5. One static global envelope and the physical trajectory

Use (4) and the finite planes (18) with sigma_i=1/M_i. Conditions
(17) hold. In coordinates 0,...,k set

    Phi_i(z)=D_i^2 phi_i((z_i-ell_i)/D_i,z_(i+1)/D_i), i<k,
    delta_tail=D_k/(1+2s_k),
    Phi_k(z)=delta_tail max{z_k-ell_k,0},
    J_sp(z)=max_(0<=i<=k) Phi_i(z),
    f(x)=min_z [J_sp(z)+||x-z||^2/2].                  (22)

This is a specified finite maximum of affine planes, including 0.
It is nonnegative and J_sp(0)=0. Its proximal minimizer is unique,
because the objective in z is coercive and strictly convex.

Here are the needed envelope properties with their proofs. Write
z=prox(x) and g=x-z; the minimizing condition is g in partial J_sp(z).
For another input x', the two subgradient inequalities give
||g-g'||^2<=<g-g',x-x'>, so the gradient candidate is 1-Lipschitz.
For an increment w, using z as a trial point gives
f(x+w)<=f(x)+<g,w>+||w||^2/2. Using the actual proximal point z'
and the subgradient inequality at z gives

    f(x+w)>=f(x)+<g,w>+||w-(z'-z)||^2/2.

These estimates prove differentiability with gradient g, and the
global lower supporting inequality proves convexity. The function is
everywhere finite, has minimum0, and has 1-Lipschitz gradient.

At the start of block i the claimed physical state is

    x_j=ell_j+D_j U_j for j<i,
    x_i=ell_i+D_i,
    x_j=0 for j>i.                                    (23)

Start from e_0 and use the local trajectory of Section4 in coordinates
i,i+1. At a candidate GLOBAL proximal point of that local trajectory,
the current Phi_i has its stated subgradient. The preceding component
has first input U_(i-1), and second input at least
ell_i/D_(i-1)>=Y_(i-1), by (21), so is0. Earlier pieces remain in
their post-checkpoint zero regions. The next component has first
input at most0 by (20) and ell_(i+1)=D_i sigma_i(1+Q_i), and second
input0. All later components, including the terminal hinge, also lie
in their pre-activation zero regions. Thus Phi_i attains the maximum
at the candidate proximal point, even in a zero-value tie. Its stated
subgradient is a subgradient of J_sp, proving that the local gradient
is the actual global gradient.

After the checkpoint, coordinate i freezes at ell_i+D_i U_i, while
coordinate i+1 is

    D_i(Y_i+sigma_i b_i)=ell_(i+1)+D_(i+1).

This proves (23) by induction through the entire supplied physical
word. No component or constraint is discarded at a transition.

During the terminal block, let u be the sum of its already executed
steps; 0<=u<=s_k. Its moving coordinate is
ell_k+D_k-u delta_tail. The proximal coordinate is at least ell_k,
since D_k-(u+1)delta_tail>=0. All old pieces vanish there. The actual
gradient is delta_tail e_k at every terminal-block query and at the
unqueried terminal point. Therefore

    f(x_n)=delta_tail D_k-(s_k+1/2)delta_tail^2
          =D_k^2/[2(1+2s_k)].                          (24)

For ALL ordered pairs of samples a,b, include the terminal and any
minimizer, and write g_a=grad f(x_a), z_a=x_a-g_a, f_a=f(x_a).
The construction gives g_a in partial J_sp(z_a) and
f_a=J_sp(z_a)+||g_a||^2/2. Exact cancellation then yields

    Q_ab=f_a-f_b+<g_a,x_b-x_a>+||g_a-g_b||^2/2
        =J_sp(z_a)-J_sp(z_b)+<g_a,z_b-z_a><=0.          (25)

For a minimizer g_star=0 and J_sp(z_star)=0, so no extra argument or
omitted optimum row is needed. Zeros give equal actual jets and also
satisfy (25). The Gram matrix is that of the explicit real vectors
in R^(k+1), so it is positive semidefinite. There is no independent
oracle branch, reset function, or unverified interpolation premise.

## 6. Exact closest radius for the single-envelope realization

Set the first two coordinates of x_star to

    (x_star)_0=1-sigma_0(epsilon+P_0),
    (x_star)_1=sigma_0(1+Q_0)=ell_1,

and all later coordinates to0. The first component is at its center,
the next has first input0 and second input0, and every later component
is in its pre-activation zero region. The terminal hinge also vanishes.
Thus J_sp(x_star)=0. Nonnegativity and attainment in (22) show that
argmin f={J_sp=0}.

Both endpoint planes of the first component have value0 at x_star.
Moreover

    e_0-x_star
      =[(P_0-epsilon Q_0)/c_sp] sigma_0(c_sp,0,0,...)
         +(1+Q_0) sigma_0(epsilon,-1,0,...).

Both coefficients are nonnegative by P_0>=epsilon Q_0. Every point
z of {J_sp=0} has nonpositive inner product with each active endpoint
slope after subtracting x_star. Hence
<e_0-x_star,z-x_star><=0. Expanding ||e_0-z||^2 proves that x_star
is the unique closest minimizer and gives the radius in (5).
Combining with (24) proves the exact payoff (6).

For epsilon<=1 and nonnegative P,Q,

    sqrt((epsilon+P)^2+(1+Q)^2)
       <=P+epsilon+(1+Q)/epsilon<=M.

Also M_i<=c_sp s_i+max{epsilon b_i,A_sp}. These two estimates give
(7). For k=0, use J_sp(z)=delta max{z,0}, delta=1/(1+2s_0),
starting at1. Its nearest minimizer is0, and the same terminal
calculation gives exactly 1/(1+2s_0).

## 7. Source-format realization, with its radius accounted for

Fix the same independent parameters and now suppose only
b_i>=2(1+epsilon^(-2)). For each i<k run Section3 on its actual
ordinary steps divided by2. In this section P_i,Q_i refer to those
HALF-step blocks, so P_i<=c_sp s_i/2 and Q_i<=H_sp. Use Section4
with local selected step beta_i=b_i/2 and

    sigma'_i=1/[c_sp s_i/2+epsilon b_i/2+A_sp].

Both conditions (17) hold because
P_i+epsilon+(1+Q_i)/epsilon<=c_sp s_i/2+A_sp.
Let phi'_i, z'_circ,i, U'_i, Y'_i be the objects in (18) for these
inputs, and put f_i=env_1 phi'_i. Define

    G_i=sigma'_i beta_i=b_i/[c_sp(a_sp+s_i)+epsilon b_i],
    D'_0=1, ell'_0=0,
    D'_(i+1)=D'_i G_i,
    ell'_(i+1)=D'_i Y'_i,
    delta'=D'_k/(1+s_k).

The single source-format objective is

    F(x)=(1/2)sum_(i<k) (D'_i)^2
               f_i((x_i-ell'_i)/D'_i,x_(i+1)/D'_i)
          +(1/2)Hub_(delta')(x_k-ell'_k),              (26)

where Hub_delta(z) is0 for z<=0, z^2/2 for 0<=z<=delta, and
delta z-delta^2/2 for z>=delta. This is the same spiral mechanism
assembled as a sum of local envelopes instead of a global envelope.

Each local term is convex and 1-smooth on its two coordinates. Summing
their quadratic upper inequalities with coefficient1/2, and adding
the terminal term, counts each coordinate at most twice. Thus

    F(x+w)<=F(x)+<grad F(x),w>+||w||^2/2.

To see directly that this proves a 1-Lipschitz gradient in this convex
case, apply the inequality to V(z)=F(z)-<grad F(y),z> at x and
w=-grad V(x). The minimum of V is at y, so

    F(x)-F(y)-<grad F(y),x-y>
       >=||grad F(x)-grad F(y)||^2/2.                  (27)

Adding the exchanged inequality and using Cauchy--Schwarz gives the
claimed Lipschitz bound. The function is nonnegative and F(0)=0 by
the zero regions and ell'_i>=0.

At the start of block i its frozen coordinates are
ell'_j+D'_j U'_j for j<i, its current coordinate is ell'_i+D'_i,
and its later coordinates are0. Along the local physical path,
x_i>=ell'_i+D'_i U'_i>=ell'_i, and
0<=x_(i+1)<=D'_i Y'_i=ell'_(i+1) before the checkpoint. Every old
local objective is therefore in its post-checkpoint zero region, and
every future one is in its pre-activation zero region. Each has zero
gradient there because it is differentiable and nonnegative. Only the
current term has a nonzero gradient. Its coefficient1/2 converts the
actual physical step h_t to the prescribed local step h_t/2. The
checkpoint leaves current coordinate ell'_i+D'_i U'_i and next
coordinate ell'_(i+1)+D'_(i+1). This proves the actual GD trajectory
by induction, using no bound on a horizontal PROXIMAL coordinate.

In the terminal block the physical gradient is delta'/2. The final
coordinate above ell'_k is D'_k-delta' s_k/2>=delta'. Substitution in
the terminal term gives

    F(x_n)=(D'_k)^2/[4(1+s_k)].                        (28)

The initial point is e_0 and 0 is an actual minimizer with distance1.
Since D'_k=product_i G_i, (28) proves the exact payoff (8) about0.
At k=0 simply use Hub_(1/(1+s_0))/2 from initial1; (28) holds with
D'_0=1. For every ordered pair of actual samples, including optimum
and terminal points, (27) with x=x_b and y=x_a is exactly Q_ab<=0.
Thus the second realization also retains every original directed row.

Here is an exact radius formula for (26); it does not replace the
simple main radius (5). Write the two coordinates of z'_circ,i as
c_(i,1) and c_(i,2). The zero set of phi'_i is exactly

    z_1<=c_(i,1),
    epsilon(z_1-c_(i,1))-(z_2-c_(i,2))<=0.             (29)

To prove equality, both inequalities are necessary because the two
endpoint slopes are included. They are sufficient because every
other (p,-q) equals a nonnegative combination of (c_sp,0) and
(epsilon,-1): its coefficients are (p-epsilon q)/c_sp and q.
The envelope has the same zero set. Since (26) is a sum of nonnegative
terms, its full minimum set is the following EXPLICIT polyhedron:

    C_rad={x: x_i<=zeta_i,
               epsilon x_i-x_(i+1)<=epsilon zeta_i-D'_i c_(i,2), i<k;
               x_k<=ell'_k},
    zeta_i=ell'_i+D'_i c_(i,1).                        (30)

It contains0. Let A_rad be the (2k+1)-by-(k+1) matrix whose first
two rows for index i are e_i^T and (epsilon e_i-e_(i+1))^T, with
right sides zeta_i and epsilon zeta_i-D'_i c_(i,2); the final row
is e_k^T with right side ell'_k. Let t_rad be this right-side column.
For a subset S of linearly independent rows, write

    A_S=(A_rad)_S, r_S=A_S e_0-(t_rad)_S,
    K_S=A_S A_S^T, lambda_S=K_S^(-1)r_S.

Include the empty subset with value0. Then the EXACT squared radius is

    rho_source^2=dist(e_0,C_rad)^2
       =max_{S independent, lambda_S>=0} r_S^T K_S^(-1)r_S. (31)

This is a finite algebraic expression determined by the given blocks.
It is not an executed active-set search or a numerical optimization.

For completeness, here is a proof of (31). For any feasible x and
any lambda>=0 on S, set w=A_S^T lambda. Then
<w,e_0-x>>=lambda^T r_S, and completing a square gives
||e_0-x||^2>=2lambda^T r_S-||w||^2. At lambda=lambda_S the right
side equals r_S^T K_S^(-1)r_S. This proves the lower bound for every
entry of the displayed maximum. Let x_* be the projection onto the
nonempty closed polyhedron C_rad, and w_*=e_0-x_*. For every direction
d with nonpositive dot product with every active row, x_*+td is
feasible for sufficiently small t>0; thus <w_*,d><=0. It follows
that w_* belongs to the nonnegative cone of the active rows. An
elementary justification is as follows. A finitely generated cone is
closed: from any nonnegative representation one can successively
remove a linear dependence while keeping coefficients nonnegative,
until only independent generators remain; it is consequently a
finite union of closed images of orthants under injective linear
maps. If w_* were outside this cone, subtracting its projection on
the cone would give a direction d with nonpositive dot product with
every generator but <w_*,d>>0, a contradiction. Choose an independent
active subset S representing w_*=A_S^T lambda with lambda>=0.
Activity gives r_S=A_S w_*=K_S lambda, hence lambda=lambda_S and
the corresponding entry in (31) equals ||w_*||^2. This proves equality.

For k>=1 the initial point is outside C_rad: its first-coordinate
constraint is 1<=c_(0,1)=1-sigma'_0(epsilon+P_0), which fails.
For k=0, C_rad is the nonpositive half-line. Therefore
0<rho_source^2<=1 in every case, the upper bound using 0 in C_rad.
The exact TRUE-distance payoff of (26) is (8)/rho_source^2. In
particular (8) is an actual payoff about0, not a quantity secretly
divided by an enlarged or unspecified radius.

## 8. Activation costs after decoupling: an exact finite family

The new range does not remove the endpoint/minimum reset cost. We
rederive a finite witness instead of importing the old unverified
obstruction. For a positive integer M put Delta=theta_sp/M and
theta_l=theta_sp-l Delta, 0<=l<=M. With A_D=A(Delta), B_D=B(Delta)
from (14), define

    t_0=0, t_l=B_D+A_D t_(l-1),
    r_l=R_sp(theta_l)[(1+t_l)e_sp(theta_l)
                                      +kappa t_l t_sp(theta_l)],
    alpha_l=1-exp(-kappa Delta)cos Delta+B_D
                         +(1+kappa^2)B_D t_(l-1).      (32)

Every alpha_l>=0 because exp(-kappa Delta)cos Delta<=1 and all
other terms are nonnegative. Resolving along e_sp(theta_l) and
t_sp(theta_l) proves exactly

    r_l-r_(l-1)=alpha_l v_sp(theta_l).

The supporting normal proves v_sp(theta_l)=Pi_(K_sp)(r_l). Thus the
ACTUAL finite chronological word (alpha_M,...,alpha_1), started at
r_M, runs backward through these indexed states to (epsilon,-1).
There is no numerical sweep or reliance on a continuous-time path.
Set S_M=sum_l alpha_l, P_M=(r_M)_1-epsilon and
Q_M=-(r_M)_2-1. Since theta_M=0,

    P_M=c_sp(1+t_M)-epsilon,
    1+Q_M=c_sp kappa t_M.                              (33)

To record exact limiting constants, define

    T_eq=[exp(d_sp theta_sp/kappa)-1]/d_sp, kappa!=1,
    T_eq=theta_sp,                                    kappa=1,
    S_eq=(1+kappa^2)(T_eq-kappa theta_sp)/d_sp, kappa!=1,
    S_eq=2theta_sp+theta_sp^2,                         kappa=1.

For every fixed positive kappa and epsilon,

    t_M -> T_eq, S_M -> S_eq.                          (34)

Here are discrete derivations of the limits. If kappa!=1 then
A_D=1+(d_sp/kappa)Delta+O(Delta^2), B_D=Delta/kappa+O(Delta^2).
For all sufficiently large M, A_D!=1, and the EXACT finite formulas
are

    t_M=B_D(A_D^M-1)/(A_D-1),
    S_M=M a_D+(1+kappa^2)B_D(t_M-M B_D)/(A_D-1),
    a_D=1-exp(-kappa Delta)cos Delta+B_D.

Using M Delta=theta_sp and A_D^M->exp(d_sp theta_sp/kappa) gives
(34), including negative d_sp. If kappa=1, then A_D=1+O(Delta^2),
B_D=Delta+O(Delta^2), and uniformly for l<=M the finite sum
t_l=B_D sum_(j=0)^(l-1) A_D^j equals l Delta+O(1/M).
Thus t_M->theta_sp and Delta sum_(l=0)^(M-1)t_l->theta_sp^2/2.
Substituting into the exact sum for S_M gives 2theta_sp+theta_sp^2.
The error constants here depend on the fixed parameters; the limits
are used to produce finite witnesses for each fixed parameter, not
as an error bound along an arbitrary growing checkpoint word.

For any local scale sigma, the endpoint plane (epsilon,-1) at the
specified zero-height origin imposes NECESSARILY

    sigma^(-1)>=epsilon+P+(1+Q)/epsilon.               (35)

This is also an original directed-row condition, not just a convenient
zero-set proof. The endpoint sample is x_end=(1-sigma P,sigma Q),
g_end=sigma(epsilon,-1), f_end=sigma^2(1+epsilon^2)/2. For prescribed
minimum data x_star=0,g_star=0,f_star=0 its original residual is

    Q_(end,star)=sigma[-epsilon
                   +sigma(epsilon^2+epsilon P+1+Q)].  (36)

It is positive precisely when (35) is violated. The extra q overshoot
or a new smoothness normalization does not cancel this row.

After paying the actual horizontal linear allowance c_sp S_M, the
finite words (32) have excess

    E_M=epsilon+P_M+(1+Q_M)/epsilon-c_sp S_M
       -> E_eq=c_sp[1+(1+kappa/epsilon)T_eq-S_eq].       (37)

Consequently any universal inverse scale c_sp S+A, with A>=0, preserving the
same endpoint and zero-height minimum requires A>=max{0,E_eq}.
With denominator c_sp S+epsilon beta+A at a fixed legal local
checkpoint beta, it requires A>=max{0,E_eq-epsilon beta}. This
accounts for the available horizontal slack BEFORE asserting a cost.
No positivity of E_eq is claimed for all large kappa.

In the useful decoupled small-curvature range
0<epsilon<=kappa<=1/2, T_eq=T_sp and (37) becomes

    E_eq=c_sp[1+(kappa/epsilon-2kappa^2/d_sp)T_sp
                         +kappa(1+kappa^2)theta_sp/d_sp]>0. (38)

The coefficient of T_sp is at least 1-2kappa^2/d_sp>=1/3.
Uniformly over 0<epsilon<=kappa as kappa tends to0,

    E_eq/[c_sp(kappa/epsilon)T_sp] -> 1,
    A_sp/[c_sp(1+kappa)T_sp/epsilon] -> 1,
    kappa A_sp/E_eq -> 1,
    kappa log(epsilon A_sp) -> pi/2,
    kappa log((epsilon/kappa)E_eq) -> pi/2.             (39)

For example the first ratio follows because the relative subtraction
2kappa epsilon/d_sp is at most2kappa^2/d_sp, and the remaining terms
are uniformly negligible compared with (kappa/epsilon)T_sp.
For the logarithmic limits, theta_sp->pi/2 uniformly in epsilon<=kappa,
log T_sp=d_sp theta_sp/kappa-log d_sp+o(1), and c_sp->1.
The displayed epsilon factors are essential: log(1/epsilon) need
not be negligible if epsilon is allowed to shrink arbitrarily fast.

Taking beta=1+epsilon^(-2) gives
epsilon beta=epsilon+1/epsilon=o(E_eq), uniformly in this small-curvature
range, since E_eq/(epsilon+1/epsilon) is asymptotic to
c_sp kappa T_sp/(1+epsilon^2), which tends to infinity.
Thus the source-format epsilon beta term does not erase this
activation obstruction. For every fixed parameter and a proposed
smaller A, sufficiently large FINITE M violates (35), hence (36).

This obstruction is confined to the same arc, endpoint jet and common
zero-height minimum condition. It does not refute different positive
heights, other curves, or the unrestricted W_n. The decoupled
construction itself remains viable because its scale pays A_sp.

## 9. Every loss, with an arbitrary number of selected checkpoints

For the single-envelope realization all paid quantities are visible:
the threshold B_sp, numerator b_i-1, linear coefficient c_sp, activation
constant A_sp, term epsilon b_i, and terminal factor 1/(1+2s_k).
Equations (20)--(21) prove that there is no extra factor involving
qmax_sp for each transition. Above kappa=1 the only new normal cost
in this realization is the explicit B_sp threshold. The exact radius
is (5); passing to (7) pays only the stated norm and displacement bounds.

When every s_i>0 for i<k, compare (7) to the FORMAL diagnostic

    I=product_i(b_i/s_i)^2/(1+2s_k).

This is not asserted to equal or control the benchmark. The exact
logarithm of I divided by (7) is

    Loss_env=2sum_i log[c_sp+max{epsilon b_i,A_sp}/s_i]
                 -2sum_i log(1-1/b_i).                (40)

It is at least2k log c_sp and at most

    2k(c_sp-1)+2sum_i max{epsilon b_i,A_sp}/s_i
                                     +2sum_i 1/(b_i-1). (41)

For the source-format payoff (8), relative to the diagnostic using
the SAME terminal factor and numerator product, the exact loss is

    Loss_source=2sum_i log[c_sp+(2A_sp+epsilon b_i)/s_i]. (42)

It is at least2k log c_sp and at most
2k(c_sp-1)+2sum_i(2A_sp+epsilon b_i)/s_i. These finite identities
are valid for arbitrary k. They do not replace a sum over checkpoints
by a single-checkpoint error or presume a uniform little-o estimate.
If a block has sum0, the actual bounds (7) and (8) remain defined;
the corresponding diagnostic with division by s_i is not used.

The exact unavoidable coefficient contribution in both diagnostics is

    2k log c_sp = k log(1+epsilon^2)+2k kappa theta_sp.   (43)

This displays the tradeoff unlocked by decoupling. For instance, along
the ONE concrete small-parameter law epsilon=kappa^2, kappa tending0,

    c_sp=1+(pi/2)kappa+O(kappa^2),
    A_sp ~ kappa^(-2) exp(pi/(2kappa)),
    E_eq ~ kappa^(-1) exp(pi/(2kappa)),
    B_sp=1+kappa^(-4).

To check the two equivalences without discarding a constant in an
exponent, use the exact identity
c_sp exp(d_sp theta_sp/kappa)=sqrt(1+epsilon^2)exp(theta_sp/kappa)
and theta_sp=pi/2-kappa^2+O(kappa^6). Formula (43) remains the
proper accumulated loss; no conclusion is drawn from its per-block
coefficient alone. These constants improve the epsilon-linked
activation exponent, but preserve an exponential in1/kappa.

Also, for ANY fixed kappa>0 and epsilon tending0, (1) has
A_sp=O_kappa(1/epsilon) while c_sp tends exp(kappa pi/2)>1.
This is a direct observation from the explicit formulas, not a
second constructed curve or a schedule experiment. Keeping curvature
fixed removes the exponential dependence on1/epsilon at the price
of a fixed coefficient loss whose product over k checkpoints is
exactly accounted for by (43). There is no uniform negligible-loss
claim over arbitrary words or checkpoint counts.

## 10. Source grounding, scope, and the remaining target

The literature grounding is Yuhan Ye and Kaizhao Liu, *Silver Rate Is
(Almost) Optimal for Gradient Descent*, arXiv:2609.09152v2, 2026,
https://arxiv.org/html/2609.09152v2, Section3.1 and AppendixA.2--A.3.
The exact HTML was downloaded and text extracted in downloads/. Its
equation (1) uses the project's W normalization. Its equation (40)
gives F(x_n)=D^2/[4(1+s_tail)] at initial distance1; retaining the
normalizing factor2 yields 1/[2(1+s_tail)], as in (8). The paper
explicitly weakens that factor in its stated Lemma2.2. This is a source
comparison only: the spiral, its local lemmas, and both global
realizations have all been derived in this joint candidate. No external
theorem is imported as a correctness premise.

Matlas returned small-step self-contracted GD, proximal curves and
projected continuous dynamics, not the present arbitrary nonnegative
explicit GD claim. Those statements are inapplicable as a substitute
for Sections2--7 and were not used. The exact primary paper supplies
the relevant assembly context, rather than any decoupled spiral theorem.

Accepted facts04219554e41d9750 and5e1848ee0b153f5f fix the original
benchmark normalization and conditional seed interfaces. They do not
prove a single lemma of this lower construction. The original ALL-split
benchmark remains

    C_0=B_0=1,
    Omega(x,y)=(x+y+sqrt((x+y)^2+4xy))/2,
    G(x,y)=2x+y/2+sqrt(y^2+8xy)/2,
    C_n=max_(0<=j<n) Omega(C_j,C_(n-j-1)),
    B_n=max_(0<=j<n) G(C_j,B_(n-j-1)),
    U_n^ZJ=1/B_n.

The unresolved ORIGINAL obligation is: for every delta>0, find a
horizon threshold independent of h such that every nonnegative word
h of every larger physical length n has a legal actual-adversary
payoff at least (1-delta)/B_n. Optimizing (6), (7), or (8) over allowed
checkpoint selections, possibly together with other accepted channels,
has not been proved to meet that obligation. The accepted phase-cover
facts and this larger parameter domain do not settle that comparison.

Outcome of this assigned round: a complete JOINT UNVERIFIED positive
extension for kappa>epsilon, in particular the full independent range
0<epsilon<=1/2, 0<kappa<=1 at unchanged threshold, and a general
kappa>0 extension with (2). The source-format sum construction works
for all positive kappa at its original doubled threshold. Activation,
transfer, smoothness, all directed rows, physical word lengths and
both radius normalizations are explicitly accounted for. No fact has
been submitted, no new result is verifier-accepted, and the original
target remains open. This consumes the one authorized analytical
round; it does not authorize self-renewal or a formal gate.

## 11. Local glossary

All symbols are defined before use above. For quick reference:
theta_sp is the endpoint angle; c_sp the initial arc radius; R_sp the
arc radial function; e_sp and t_sp its radial and clockwise tangent
unit vectors; v_sp its supporting gradient; n_sp its supporting normal;
K_sp its convex hull with0; p,q its two coordinate magnitudes;
qmax_sp its exact vertical maximum; N_sp the certified horizontal
projection deficit; B_sp the sufficient physical checkpoint threshold;
T_sp the normal-parameter upper bound; H_sp the vertical displacement
allowance; A_sp the additive activation allowance; a_sp its source
format rescaling. The normal coefficient t_j is unrelated to t_sp.
P,Q are actual ordinary-block displacements and s_i their physical
step sums. M_i,sigma_i,D_i,ell_i specify the single-envelope scales
and shifts; J_sp is its finite nonsmooth maximum and f its envelope.
Primed quantities, G_i,F and Hub_delta belong to the second realization.
C_rad,A_rad,t_rad,r_S,K_S,lambda_S,rho_source specify its exact
polyhedral closest-radius formula. T_eq,S_eq,E_eq are the limiting
constants of the explicitly defined finite equal-angle family;
they are not numerical evaluations. Q_ab denotes the original ordered
smooth-convex residual. I and the two Loss quantities are diagnostic
comparisons, not the Zhang--Jiang benchmark. C_n,B_n,U_n^ZJ,W_n and R_n
retain their existing project meanings.
